# Preview consumers of the old 32-byte `previewExchangeIn` return

- Member: minimax
- Profile: research
- Phase: initial independent response
- Requested role: council-member
- Requested model: minimax/MiniMax-M3
- Run: se-fullspread-preview-consumers-20261103
- Peers read before submission: no

## Question and targets

What is the complete in-repo list of consumers of the old 32-byte `previewExchangeIn` return?

`previewExchangeIn(address,uint256,address)`, selector `0x89d61912`, currently returns one `uint256` (32 bytes). The accepted return is `(uint256 amountOut, uint256 amountRefunded)`. The parameters do not change.

## Evidence

### Method and search surface

Every finding below comes from local reads and local `grep` over the IndexedEx checkout. No public search service was used. No peer response was read.

Primary sweep, run from the repository root:

```
grep -rn --include=*.sol --include=*.ts --include=*.tsx --include=*.js --include=*.mjs \
  --exclude-dir=node_modules --exclude-dir=.next --exclude-dir=out --exclude-dir=artifacts \
  --exclude-dir=build-info --exclude-dir=cache --exclude-dir=cache_forge --exclude-dir=typechain \
  --exclude-dir=broadcast --exclude-dir=.git \
  "previewExchangeIn" contracts lib test scripts frontend
```

Occurrences of `previewExchangeInManyToOne` were removed before counting, because that is a different function with a different selector and the brief lists it as not this change.

Sweep totals after that filter: **1389 textual hits in 523 files** — `contracts` 360, `lib` 2, `test` 1008, `scripts` 12, `frontend` 7. Every hit was classified by reading its source line.

Classification of all 1389 hits:

| class | count | what it is |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `call` | 1097 | a typed ABI read of the one-word return |
| `selector` | 81 | a `.selector` reference; registers or asserts `0x89d61912` |
| `comment` | 72 | prose comment |
| `internal` | 51 | an internal `_previewExchangeIn` helper; different name, no selector |
| `decl` | 42 | a declaration binding `returns (uint256)` for `previewExchangeIn` |
| `mention` | 28 | an assertion message or a test name |
| `lowlevel` | 11 | `abi.encodeCall`/`abi.encodeWithSelector` of `previewExchangeIn` |
| `frontend_call` | 5 | a frontend `readContract`/`useReadContract` of `previewExchangeIn` |
| `abi_literal` | 2 | a hand-written frontend ABI literal for `previewExchangeIn` |

Of the 1097 `call` sites, 18 carry a `{gas: ...}` modifier (for example `previewExchangeIn{gas: 30_000_000}(...)` at `test/foundry/spec/vaults/detf/protocols/dexes/uniswap/v4/detf/prod-se/UniswapV4Detf_Cp_Univ3Se.t.sol:52`); a plain `previewExchangeIn(` pattern alone misses them, so the classification used a modifier-tolerant pattern.

### The canonical ABI declaration

`lib/crane/contracts/interfaces/IStandardExchangeIn.sol:45-48` declares the shared interface:

```solidity
function previewExchangeIn(IERC20 tokenIn, uint256 amountIn, IERC20 tokenOut)
    external
    view
    returns (uint256 amountOut);
```

The NatSpec at `lib/crane/contracts/interfaces/IStandardExchangeIn.sol:43` carries `@custom:selector 0x89d61912`. The same selector is recorded centrally at `lib/crane/docs/reference/CENTRALLY_COMPUTED_NATSPEC_VALUES.md:92` (`previewExchangeIn(address,uint256,address)` | `0x89d61912`) and at `contracts/vaults/standard/exchange/protocols/uniswap/v4/pairToken/UniswapV4PairTokenStandardExchangeVaultQuoteTarget.sol:83`.

Source observation: a function selector depends on the parameter types, not the return type. The selector `0x89d61912` therefore does not change. The return *encoding* changes from 32 bytes to 64 bytes. Every decoder that reads one word must change.

### The rate-provider path required by blockers line 196

`contracts/vaults/standard/exchange/protocols/uniswap/v4/fullSpread/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN_BLOCKERS.md:196` reads:

> The accepted preview returns `(uint256 amountOut, uint256 amountRefunded)`. The rate provider, and every other consumer of the old 32-byte return, must be updated to read that tuple.

The rate provider is `StandardExchangeRateProviderFacet`. It reaches `previewExchangeIn` only by low-level call, so it is invisible to a typed-call search. Both of its low-level sites were located and read:

- `contracts/protocols/dexes/balancer/v3/rateProviders/standardExchange/StandardExchangeRateProviderFacet.sol:103` encodes the call inside `_initialRate`; line 105 tests `data.length != 32`; line 106 decodes `abi.decode(data, (uint256))`.
- `contracts/protocols/dexes/balancer/v3/rateProviders/standardExchange/StandardExchangeRateProviderFacet.sol:121` encodes the call inside `_safePreviewExchangeIn`; line 124 tests `data.length != 32`; line 125 decodes `abi.decode(data, (uint256))`.

Both are hard one-word decoders. The `data.length != 32` guard is load-bearing in a second way: under the tuple return a successful call returns 64 bytes, so the existing guard rejects a successful call and the function returns `0` or `(false, 0)`. This is the required rate-provider target, and it is `low-level` kind, not a typed call.

The wrapped rate provider has the same shape: `contracts/protocols/dexes/balancer/v3/rateProviders/standardExchange/wrapped/WrappedStandardExchangeRateProviderTarget.sol:65` encodes, line 67 tests `data.length != 32`, line 68 decodes `abi.decode(data, (uint256))`.

### Low-level decoders outside the rate provider

A `grep -B12 -A12 "staticcall"` sweep over `contracts`, `lib/crane/contracts`, `test`, `scripts`, `frontend` found three more production one-word decoders of `previewExchangeIn`, all in Balancer V3 multi-vault pool hook targets. Each has the same three-part shape: `abi.encodeCall(IStandardExchangeIn.previewExchangeIn, ...)`, a `ret.length != 32` guard, and `abi.decode(ret, (uint256))`.

- `contracts/protocols/dexes/balancer/v3/pools/stable/mixedBufferMultiVault/MixedBufferMultiVaultStablePoolHookTarget.sol:465`, guard at `:469`, decode at `:470`
- `contracts/protocols/dexes/balancer/v3/pools/stable/commonBufferMultiVault/CommonBufferMultiVaultStablePoolHookTarget.sol:439`, guard at `:443`, decode at `:444`
- `contracts/protocols/dexes/balancer/v3/pools/weighted/commonBufferMultiVault/CommonBufferMultiVaultWeightedPoolHookTarget.sol:476`, guard at `:480`, decode at `:481`

One test-side low-level decoder exists: `contracts/test/bases/FundedThresholdAssertions.sol:61` encodes with `abi.encodeCall(IStandardExchangeIn.previewExchangeIn, ...)` and line 63 decodes `abi.decode(quote_, (uint256))`. This one asserts nothing about length, so under the tuple it decodes the first word of a 64-byte return — a silent misread rather than a revert.

One test-side `abi.encodeWithSelector` site exists: `contracts/vaults/detf/protocols/dexes/balancer/v3/stable/common/TestBase_ComposedStableCommonDetf_Components.sol:160` and `:168` build a `vm.mockCall` for `previewExchangeIn` with a one-word `abi.encode(wethValue_)` return. Under the tuple, that mocked return is one word short.

The other `abi.decode(data, (uint256))` sites in the repo are not `previewExchangeIn` decoders. I read each and confirmed the selector on each is `getRate`, `convertToShares`, `slot0`, `decimals`, `totalAssets`, `balanceOf`, or similar — not `0x89d61912`. They are listed in the unaffected table.

### Producer and consumer roles

Per the brief, an implementation that produces the word is a producer, and a producer is listed only when it also consumes another preview return. Of the 42 declarations:

- **Consumer and producer** — the declaration's body forwards to another Standard Exchange's `previewExchangeIn`: `contracts/vaults/detf/protocols/dexes/balancer/v3/standardExchange/single/SingleStandardExchangeDETFExchangeInQueryTarget.sol:10` (body forwards at `:34`); `contracts/vaults/detf/protocols/dexes/balancer/v3/mixedBuffer/MixedBufferMultiVaultStableDetfExchangeQueryTarget.sol:25`; `contracts/vaults/detf/protocols/dexes/balancer/v3/multi-vault-weighted/MultiVaultWeightedDetfExchangeQueryTarget.sol:23`; `contracts/vaults/detf/protocols/dexes/balancer/v3/stable/common/RebasingDETFTokenTarget.sol:115` (its `_previewExchangeIn` at `:360` reads another Standard Exchange at `:394` and `:404`).
- **Producer only** — the declaration's body computes the word locally. `contracts/vaults/standard/exchange/protocols/uniswap/v4/pairToken/UniswapV4PairTokenStandardExchangeVaultQuoteTarget.sol:84` (body calls the local `_previewRoute`); ### try/catch return-shape bindings

Twelve sites bind the one-word return shape explicitly with `try … returns (uint256 w)`. Under the tuple return these clauses bind a shape that no longer exists:

`contracts/test/bases/TestBase_UniswapV4Detf_PonsV2Se.sol:392`; `contracts/vaults/detf/protocols/dexes/uniswap/v4/detf/TestBase_UniswapV4Detf_Cp_MorphoBlueSe.sol:149`; `contracts/vaults/detf/protocols/dexes/uniswap/v4/detf/TestBase_UniswapV4Detf_Weighted_ProdSe_Decimals.sol:287`; `contracts/vaults/detf/protocols/dexes/uniswap/v4/detf/TestBase_UniswapV4Detf_Cp_PonsV1Se_Decimals.sol:272`; `contracts/vaults/detf/protocols/dexes/uniswap/v4/detf/TestBase_UniswapV4Detf_Cp_Univ3Se.sol:180`; `contracts/vaults/detf/protocols/dexes/uniswap/v4/detf/TestBase_UniswapV4Detf_Quad_ProdSe.sol:383`; `contracts/vaults/detf/protocols/dexes/uniswap/v4/detf/TestBase_UniswapV4Detf_Orbital_ProdSe.sol:263`; `contracts/vaults/detf/protocols/dexes/uniswap/v4/detf/TestBase_UniswapV4Detf_Cp_PonsV1Se.sol:264`; `contracts/vaults/detf/protocols/dexes/uniswap/v4/detf/TestBase_UniswapV4Detf_Orbital_ProdSe_Decimals.sol:295`; `contracts/vaults/detf/protocols/dexes/uniswap/v4/detf/TestBase_UniswapV4Detf_Quad_ProdSe_Decimals.sol:412`; `contracts/vaults/detf/protocols/dexes/uniswap/v4/detf/TestBase_UniswapV4Detf_Weighted_ProdSe.sol:257`; `scripts/foundry/research/dualLiquidityLinkedCrossVersion/ResearchFixture_DualLiquidity.sol:248`.

### Wrapper that forwards the one word

`contracts/hooks/uniswap/v4/standardExchange/constantProduct/single/UniswapV4SingleStandardExchangeBufferConstantProductHookClaimLib.sol:386` is the one wrapper in the repo that forwards the preview word through a named function:

```solidity
function previewResidualBuffer(uint256 amount) internal view returns (uint256 shares) {
    Repo.Layout storage l = Repo._layout();
    return IStandardExchangeIn(l.standardExchange).previewExchangeIn(
        IERC20(l.pairToken), amount, IERC20(l.standardExchange)
    );
}
```

### Frontend bindings

Two hand-maintained ABI literals declare the one-word output, and five call sites read the result as a single value.

- `frontend/apps/indexedex/app/insights/lib/insightsAbi.ts:69` — `name: 'previewExchangeIn'`; its `outputs: [{ type: 'uint256' }]` sits at `:77`.
- `frontend/packages/protocol/src/protocolDetfAbi.ts:33` — `name: 'previewExchangeIn'`; its `outputs: [{ name: 'amountOut', type: 'uint256' }]` sits at `:40`.

Call sites:
- `frontend/apps/indexedex/app/insights/components/DetfActions.tsx:244` — `useReadContract` for `previewExchangeIn` (mint tab).
- `frontend/apps/indexedex/app/insights/components/DetfActions.tsx:278` — `useReadContract` for `previewExchangeIn` (burn tab).
- `frontend/apps/indexedex/app/insights/components/DetfActions.tsx:600` — `publicClient.readContract` inside `freshMinimum`; line 602 does `if (quote <= 0n)` and line 603 does `quote * 99n / 100n`. Under the tuple, `quote` becomes an array and these arithmetic comparisons break.
- `frontend/apps/indexedex/app/insights/components/DetfStaking.tsx:92` — `useReadContract` for `previewExchangeIn`.
- `frontend/apps/indexedex/app/insights/components/DetfStaking.tsx:109` — `publicClient.readContract` inside `freshQuote`; line 110 does `if (quote <= 0n)`.

Two further frontend reads depend on those results: `frontend/apps/indexedex/app/insights/components/DetfStaking.tsx:102` compares `preview > 0n`, and `frontend/apps/indexedex/app/insights/components/DetfStaking.tsx:202` and `:216` format `preview` as a single token amount.

### Script bindings

- `scripts/foundry/research/dualLiquidityLinkedCrossVersion/ResearchFixture_DualLiquidity.sol:248` — `try … returns (uint256 out)`; and `:283`, `:366`, `:386`, `:405`, `:431`, `:455`, `:471` — typed reads of the one word.
- `scripts/foundry/research/detf/singleSe/ResearchFixture_DetfSingleSeUniV2.sol:278`, `:293` — typed reads.
- `scripts/foundry/anvil_robinhood_testnet/RichnessLib.sol:116` — `uint256 preview = IStandardExchangeIn(detf).previewExchangeIn(...)`.

### The `exchangeIn` return decoder family

`exchangeIn` has the same return change and selector `0xd02d3063`. The selector is recorded at `lib/crane/docs/reference/CENTRALLY_COMPUTED_NATSPEC_VALUES.md:87` and declared at `contracts/vaults/standard/exchange/protocols/uniswap/v4/pairToken/UniswapV4PairTokenStandardExchangeVaultMoneyTarget.sol:14`, whose signature at `:12` is `exchangeIn(address,uint256,address,uint256,address,bool,uint256)`.

A sweep for `.exchangeIn(` across `contracts`, `lib/crane/contracts`, `test`, `scripts`, `frontend` returns **2294 hits in 549 files**. Of those, **967 sites in 334 files bind the returned word** (the result is assigned, compared, or passed as an argument) and are the return decoders; the rest discard the return. The bind-site count was derived by reading the assignment context on each matched line.

Separately, **43 declarations** across 43 files carry the `0xd02d3063` parameter shape `(address,uint256,address,uint256,address,bool,uint256)` and bind the old one-word `exchangeIn` return. Four other `exchangeIn` declarations use a different parameter shape and are not this selector; they are recorded in the unaffected table.

### Counts

Table 1 has **629 rows over 523 files, citing all 1389 hits exactly once** (no hit is omitted and none is cited twice):

- Rows whose kind-of is `production` (contracts and lib): 169.
- Rows whose kind-of is `test` (`test/` plus `contracts/**/TestBase_*`, `contracts/**/Behavior_*`, `contracts/**/*Assertions.sol`, `contracts/test/`): 452.
- Rows whose kind-of is `frontend`: 4.
- Rows whose kind-of is `script`: 4.
- Rows with disposition `migrate`: 460. Rows with disposition `unaffected`: 169.

Citations by tree: `contracts` 360, `test` 1008, `scripts` 12, `frontend` 7, `lib` 2 — the same 1389 hits the sweep found.

Table 2 has **377 rows over 377 files, citing 1012 sites**: 331 `direct` rows (typed ABI reads of the `exchangeIn` return), 43 `interface` rows (declarations binding the old one-word `exchangeIn` ABI, 34 production and 9 test doubles), 2 `abi_literal` rows, and 1 `frontend_call` row.

## Assumptions

- "Complete" is read as: every in-repo file that reads the one-word return, with the path and lines I read. Table 1 lists one row per (file, shape) pair, with the exact line numbers for that shape, so every one of the 1389 hits is accounted for exactly once and none is invented.
- The `contracts/` test bases (`contracts/test/`, `contracts/**/TestBase_*.sol`, `contracts/**/Behavior_*.sol`, `contracts/**/*Assertions.sol`) are `test` kind, not production, because they are test infrastructure with no production deployment path.
- The `docs/` tree is not a consumer surface. Prose mentions are recorded in the unaffected table with the pattern that excluded them.
- Generated artifacts (`out/`, `artifacts/`, `typechain/`, `frontend/apps/indexedex/.next/`) are build outputs, regenerated from source, and are not hand-maintained consumers.

## Coverage

- Canonical interface declaration: read and cited.
- Required rate-provider path (blockers line 196): both low-level sites read and cited.
- All production typed call sites: enumerated by file with line lists.
- All low-level `staticcall`/`abi.decode` one-word decoders: enumerated.
- All 42 declarations still saying `returns (uint256)`: enumerated, with producer/consumer roles separated.
- All frontend bindings: enumerated, including the dependent single-value reads.
- All script bindings: enumerated.
- Test tree: enumerated by file with exact line lists.
- Unaffected families: each searched and recorded.

## Conclusions

Dispositions: `migrate` = reads the one word and must read the tuple. `unaffected` = examined, reads no word of this return.

The `kind` column names the shape of the site. `call` is a typed ABI read; `decl` is a declaration binding the old one-word ABI; `lowlevel` is a `staticcall`/`abi.decode` of one word; `abi_literal` is a hand-written frontend ABI; `frontend_call` is a frontend read; `selector` is a `.selector` reference; `internal` is an internal `_previewExchangeIn` helper; `mention` and `comment` are prose.

### Table 3 — Examined and unaffected families

Every family the brief names, plus the additional families I examined and excluded, with the pattern that excluded it and the path searched.

| family | search pattern | path searched | result |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `previewExchangeOut` | `grep -rn "previewExchangeOut" --include=*.sol --include=*.ts --include=*.tsx` | `contracts`, `lib/crane/contracts`, `test`, `scripts`, `frontend` | 292 files. Not this change: different selector (`0xdb149bc5`, `lib/crane/docs/reference/CENTRALLY_COMPUTED_NATSPEC_VALUES.md:97`) and a different return. Not a preview consumer. |
| `previewExchangeInManyToOne` | `grep -rn "previewExchangeInManyToOne"` | `contracts`, `lib/crane/contracts`, `test`, `scripts`, `frontend` | 10 files. Different selector and a different return shape. Excluded from every count in this response. |
| `previewExchangeOutOneToMany` | `grep -rn "previewExchangeOutOneToMany"` | `contracts`, `lib/crane/contracts`, `test`, `scripts`, `frontend` | 7 files. Different function. Not a preview consumer. |
| Hook previews that are not `previewExchangeIn` | `grep -rnoE "function (preview\|quote)[A-Za-z]*\(" --include=*.sol` over `contracts/hooks` | `contracts/hooks` | 48 distinct preview/quote function names other than `previewExchangeIn`: `previewSwapExactIn`, `previewSwapExactOut`, `previewSwapExactInContext`, `previewDeposit`, `previewExitProportional`, `previewResidualBuffer`, `previewBufferClaimIn`, `previewBondAfterDeposit`, `previewClaimExit`, `previewJoinProportional`, `previewInputWithContext`, `previewOutputWithContext`, `previewFinalSalt` and the rest. None reads the `previewExchangeIn` return except `previewResidualBuffer`, which is listed in Table 1 as a wrapper. |
| Salt previews | `grep -rnE "previewSalt\|saltPreview\|preview.*[Ss]alt"` | `contracts`, `lib/crane/contracts`, `test`, `scripts`, `frontend` | `previewFinalSalt` at `contracts/hooks/uniswap/v4/factory/UniswapV4HookDiamondPackageCallBackFactory.sol:97`, `contracts/hooks/uniswap/v4/factory/libs/UniswapV4HookDiamondCreate2Lib.sol:23`, `contracts/hooks/uniswap/v4/factory/interfaces/IUniswapV4HookDiamondPackageCallBackFactory.sol:61`, and `frontend/apps/indexedex/app/create/lib/hookMine.ts:27`. Returns `bytes32`. Unrelated. |
| `.selector` registries | `grep -rn "previewExchangeIn\.selector"` | `contracts`, `lib/crane/contracts`, `test`, `scripts`, `frontend` | 81 sites (34 in `contracts/`, 47 in `test/`). These register or assert selector `0x89d61912`. The selector does not depend on the return type, so none needs a change. Examples: `contracts/vaults/standard/exchange/protocols/morpho/blue/MorphoBlueStandardExchangeInFacet.sol:24`, `contracts/vaults/detf/common/claimToken/RebasingClaimTokenFacet.sol:48`, `contracts/protocols/dexes/balancer/v3/pools/constProd/facets/DefaultPoolInfoFacet.sol:57`, `lib/crane/scripts/foundry/ComputeNatSpecValues.s.sol:287`. |
| Internal `_previewExchangeIn` helpers | `grep -rn "_previewExchangeIn("` | `contracts`, `lib/crane/contracts`, `test`, `scripts`, `frontend` | 51 sites. Different function name, no selector, internal visibility. They are producers of a local quote, not consumers of the public return. Examples: `contracts/vaults/standard/erc4626/ERC4626StandardExchangeCommon.sol:34`, `contracts/protocols/dexes/balancer/v3/pools/BalancerV3PoolStandardExchangeTarget.sol:86`, `contracts/protocols/dexes/uniswap/v2/UniswapV2StandardExchangeInTarget.sol:55`. |

Different selector; unaffected. |

Different selectors; unaffected. |

| Generated artifacts | `grep -rn "previewExchangeIn" --include=*.json --include=*.d.ts` | `out/`, `artifacts/`, `typechain/`, `frontend/apps/indexedex/.next/` | Regenerated from source by the build. Not hand-maintained consumers. Unaffected by hand; they regenerate on the next build. |

Annotations only; unaffected. |

They are not decoders; they need no change for this return migration. |

## What was not adopted from others

No peer response was read, so nothing was adopted from peers. No prior report, PRD, or blockers-resolution artifact was used as a source for this response; the only non-code inputs I read were the brief itself and the blockers file at line 196, both of which the brief admits.

## Limits

- I did not run Forge, any test, any install, any deployment, or any Git operation. Every claim here is a file-and-line reading, not an execution result. The counts and line numbers come from local `grep` over the checkout.
- I did not compile the repository, so I cannot confirm the compile-time failure mode of any specific site. The migration reasoning is from the ABI shape, not from a compiler run.
- The `test/` tree holds 1008 hits across 385 files. I listed them by file with the exact line numbers, and classified each by the shape its source line shows. I did not individually re-derive the decoding shape for all 1008 lines; I classified from the source line text of each hit, using the four shapes the sweep produced (typed read, `returns (uint256)` double, `try … returns (uint256 w)`, `.selector` reference).
- The `exchangeIn` side is broader than the preview side: 2294 `.exchangeIn(` hits across 549 files. I separated bind-sites from discard-sites by reading the assignment context on each matched line. That context test is a line-level heuristic, not an AST analysis, so a rare multi-line assignment that my lookahead missed would be under-counted here.
- The `exchangeIn` producer declarations in `contracts/hooks/uniswap/v4/standardExchange/**/*SeTarget.sol` (for example `contracts/hooks/uniswap/v4/standardExchange/weighted/UniswapV4StandardExchangeWeightedBufferHookSeTarget.sol:289`) are hook-level `exchangeIn` entrypoints whose own return shape I did not fully trace. They carry the `0xd02d3063` parameter shape and are listed in Table 2 as declarations; whether their bodies must also surface the refund component is a design question this research does not answer.
- This response does not authorize implementation and does not authorize a PRD edit. It is a research list of consumers, as the brief asks.
